<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the bond-percolation nonuniqueness conjecture of Benjamini and Schramm: every infinite connected, locally finite, nonamenable quasi-transitive graph has a nonempty interval of parameters for which Bernoulli bond percolation almost surely has infinitely many infinite clusters. We also prove Hutchcroft's stronger operator-threshold conjecture, establishing $`p_c\lt p_{2\to2}\le p_u`$.

- **claim-main-gap-and-interval** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph with $h_V(G)>0$. For Bernoulli bond percolation on $G$, $$\sup_{0\le p<{p_c}}\lVert T_p\rVert_{2\to2} =\lVert T_{{p_c}}\rVert_{2\to2}<\infty, \qquad {p_c}(G)<{p_{2\to 2}}(G)\le{p_u}(G).$$ There exist ${p_c}<p_1<p_2<1$ such that, in the coupling obtained from independent uniform edge labels, almost surely there are infinitely many infinite open clusters simultaneously for every $p\in[p_1,p_2]$.
  - Explanation: For infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive nonamenable graphs, the connection operator at the critical parameter is bounded and equals the supremum of the subcritical norms. The critical parameter lies strictly below the operator threshold, which is at most the uniqueness threshold, and one coupling gives infinitely many infinite clusters throughout a compact interval above criticality.
- **claim-cayley-arbitrary-generators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-cayley)): Let $\Lambda$ be a nonamenable finitely generated group, and let $S$ be any finite symmetric generating set with the identity removed. For Bernoulli bond percolation on the simple undirected Cayley graph with edges $\{x,xs\}$, $x\in\Lambda$, $s\in S$, the critical connection operator is bounded on counting-measure $\ell^2(\Lambda)$ and $${p_c}<{p_{2\to 2}}\le{p_u}.$$ The simultaneous nonuniqueness interval in <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> exists for every such fixed generating set. At every fixed $p\in({p_c},{p_u})$, there are almost surely infinitely many infinite open clusters.
  - Explanation: The main theorem applies to the simple Cayley graph of a nonamenable finitely generated group for every finite symmetric generating set without the identity, giving critical operator boundedness, the strict threshold gap and infinitely many infinite clusters inside the nonuniqueness phase.
- **claim-critical-laws-exponential-connectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-critical-laws)): Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph with $h_V(G)>0$. For Bernoulli bond percolation on $G$, $$\label{eq:critical-triangle} \nabla_{{p_c}}(v)\le\lVert T_{{p_c}}\rVert_{2\to2}^{3}<\infty \qquad\text{for every }v\in V.$$ For every $v\in V$, $$\begin{aligned} \chi_v(p)&\asymp({p_c}-p)^{-1} &&(p\uparrow{p_c}), \label{eq:meanfield-chi}\\ \theta_v(p)&\asymp p-{p_c}&&(p\downarrow{p_c}), \label{eq:meanfield-theta}\\ \mathbb P_{{p_c}}(\|C_v\|\ge n)&\asymp n^{-1/2} &&(n\to\infty), \label{eq:meanfield-size}\\ \mathbb P_{{p_c}}\bigl(\operatorname{rad}_{\mathrm{int}}(C_v)\ge n\bigr) &\asymp n^{-1} &&(n\to\infty), \label{eq:meanfield-intrinsic}\\ \mathbb P_{{p_c}}\bigl(\operatorname{rad}_{\mathrm{ext}}(C_v)\ge n\bigr) &\asymp n^{-1} &&(n\to\infty). \label{eq:meanfield-extrinsic} \end{aligned}$$ Here $n$ tends through positive integers. The comparison constants and the one-sided neighborhoods of ${p_c}$ may depend on $G$ and $v$. Moreover, $$\label{eq:exponential-threshold} {p_c}(G)<{p_{2\to 2}}(G)\le{p_{\exp}}(G).$$ For each fixed $p\in[0,{p_{2\to 2}}(G))$, there are constants $0<A_{G,p}<\infty$ and $a_{G,p}>0$ such that $$\label{eq:exponential-connectivity} T_p(x,y)\le A_{G,p}e^{-a_{G,p}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_G(x,y)} \qquad\text{for every }x,y\in V.$$
  - Explanation: Critical operator boundedness makes the triangle diagram finite at criticality and yields the mean-field laws for susceptibility, percolation probability, cluster size and both radii. It also gives exponential decay of connection probabilities below the operator threshold, which exceeds the critical parameter.
- **lemma-simple-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-simple-reduction)): It suffices to prove the operator gap and the infinite-cluster conclusion for simple graphs satisfying the hypotheses of <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.
  - Explanation: Loops and multiple edges can be removed by subdividing edges, so it is enough to prove the operator gap and the infinite-cluster conclusion for simple graphs.
- **lemma-critical-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-critical-finiteness)): We have $0<{p_c}<1$. At ${p_c}$, all open clusters are finite almost surely, simultaneously at every vertex and in every induced subgraph under restriction of the full configuration.
  - Explanation: The critical parameter is nontrivial, and at the critical parameter every open cluster is almost surely finite, simultaneously at all vertices and in all induced subgraphs.
- **lemma-association-disjoint-witness-tree** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-probability-tools)): Increasing nonnegative functions of independent percolation bits have nonnegative covariance. If increasing events have disjoint finite open witnesses, their joint disjoint-occurrence probability is at most the product of their probabilities. In particular, for all $x,y,z$, $$\label{eq:s01} \mathbb P_p(x\leftrightarrow y,\ x\leftrightarrow z) \le\sum_w T_p(x,w)T_p(w,y)T_p(w,z).$$ The same assertions hold in every induced domain.
  - Explanation: Product-measure correlation tools: positive association of increasing functions, the disjoint-witness inequality, and the three-terminal tree bound, all valid in induced domains.
- **lemma-threshold-order** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-threshold-order)): For every quasi-transitive graph under consideration, ${p_{2\to 2}}\le{p_u}$.
  - Explanation: The operator threshold cannot exceed the uniqueness threshold, since almost-sure uniqueness forces connection probabilities bounded below and thus unbounded operators.
- **prop-unimodular-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-unimodular-reduction)): If $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(G)$ is nonunimodular, then ${p_c}<{p_{2\to 2}}\le{p_u}$ and $T_{p_c}$ is bounded. For the proof of the operator gap it therefore remains to treat the case in which $\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(G)$ is unimodular.
  - Explanation: When the automorphism group is nonunimodular the strict gap and critical boundedness follow from an existing operator theorem, so only the unimodular case needs new work.
- **thm-critical-operator-unimodular** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-critical-operator)): Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite simple quasi-transitive graph with $h_V(G)>0$. If $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(G)$ is unimodular, then $\lVert T_{{p_c}}\rVert_{2\to2}<\infty$.
  - Explanation: For a simple nonamenable quasi-transitive graph with unimodular automorphism group, the critical connection operator is bounded on counting-measure square-summable functions. This is the principal new estimate.
- **lemma-weighted-mass-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-mass-transport)): For every invariant nonnegative function $m$ on ordered vertex pairs, $$\label{eq:s02} \left[\sum_y m(o,y)\right] =\left[\sum_x m(x,o)\right].$$ The identity also applies to the expectation of a covariant random mass and to absolutely summable signed or complex masses. In particular, an invariant stochastic kernel on a single orbit has column sums one, whether or not it is symmetric.
  - Explanation: Type-weighted mass transport: total outgoing and incoming masses agree for invariant nonnegative functions, and the same identity extends to random and absolutely summable masses.
- **lemma-invariant-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-trace)): For a bounded invariant operator $S$, define $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S=[S(o,o)]$. This is a positive normalized trace: $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}I=1$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(AB)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(BA)$ for bounded invariant $A,B$. If $A$ is positive semidefinite, invariant, and bounded, and $B=B^*$ is invariant and bounded, then $$\|\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(BA)\|\le\lVert B\rVert\,\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A, \qquad A(x,x)\le c_\sigma\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A.$$
  - Explanation: The diagonal type average is a normalized positive trace with cyclicity on bounded invariant operators, with a trace estimate for products with positive semidefinite operators.
- **lemma-walk-spectral-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-walk)): The kernel $P$ is positive semidefinite and $$\label{eq:s03} \rho:=\lVert P\rVert<1.$$ Its projection onto the vertex types is an irreducible lazy finite Markov chain. There exist $c_*>0$ and $l_0<\infty$ such that $$\sum_{y\in O_j}P^l(x,y)\ge c_* \quad\text{for every }x\in V,\ j\le h_0,\ l\ge l_0.$$
  - Explanation: The symmetric lazy walk kernel is positive semidefinite with operator norm strictly below one, and its type projection is an irreducible lazy finite chain with uniformly positive long-time type probabilities.
- **lemma-subcritical-susceptibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-sharpness)): For $p<{p_c}$, $\chi_{\max}(p)<\infty$ and $$\sup_x\mathbb E_p s_x^2\le\chi_{\max}(p)^3, \qquad \chi_{\max}(p)\le c_\sigma\chi(p).$$ There is $c>0$, depending only on $G$, such that $\chi_x(p)\ge c\chi(p)$ for every $x$ and ${p_c}/2\le p<{p_c}$. The operator $T_p$ is symmetric, positive semidefinite, and bounded with $\lVert T_p\rVert\le\chi_{\max}(p)$.
  - Explanation: Below criticality the maximal susceptibility is finite, second cluster moments are controlled by its cube, susceptibilities at different vertices are comparable, and the connection operator is symmetric, positive semidefinite and bounded by the susceptibility.
- **lemma-russo-operator-norm** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-russo)): The map $p\mapsto T_p$ is locally absolutely continuous in operator norm on $(0,{p_c})$, and $\chi$ is locally absolutely continuous there. For almost every $p\in(0,{p_c})$, $$\label{eq:s04} \begin{aligned} (1-p)T'_p(x,y) &=\sum_a\sum_{b\sim a} \mathbb P_p(x\in C_a,\ y\in C_b,\ C_a\ne C_b),\\ (1-p)\chi' &=\left[\sum_{b\sim o} \mathbb E_p(s_o s_b;\ C_o\ne C_b)\right]. \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The connection operator and the type-averaged susceptibility are locally absolutely continuous below criticality, with Russo-type formulas for their derivatives in terms of distinct neighbouring clusters.
- **lemma-sprinkling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-sprinkling)): For $0\le p,a\le1$, $$\label{eq:s05} T_{p+(1-p)a} \le\sum_{j\ge0}a^jT_p(\mathop{\mathrm{Adj}}T_p)^j \qquad\text{entrywise}.$$ If $T_p$ is bounded and $ad\lVert T_p\rVert<1$, the kernel on the left is bounded. In particular, boundedness of $T_{p_c}$ implies ${p_c}<{p_{2\to 2}}$.
  - Explanation: Entrywise comparison of the connection kernel at a larger parameter with a series in the kernel at a smaller one; boundedness at criticality therefore propagates to a larger parameter and gives the strict gap.
- **prop-good-critical-clusters** (bound, [source](#stmt-good-good-clusters)): For each $\epsilon\in(0,1/2)$, uniformly in $x$ and for all sufficiently large $M$, $$\label{eq:s06} \mathbb P_{p_c}(s_x\le M,\ C_x\text{ is not good for cap }M) \le e^{-M^\epsilon}.$$ The event that a cluster has size at most $M$ and is good for cap $M$ is determined by the percolation bits in a deterministic finite-radius ball about its root.
  - Explanation: A capped critical cluster fails to be good with stretched-exponentially small probability, and goodness together with the cap is decided by bits in a deterministic finite ball.
- **lemma-marked-pivotal-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-good-marked-pivotals)): There is a constant $C$, depending only on $d$ and ${p_c}$, such that, with $A_h=C\sqrt{\log(1/h)/h}\ge1$, for every induced domain $D$, every $x\in D$, every integer $i\ge0$, and every $r\ge0$, $$\label{eq:s07} \begin{aligned} \mathbb E\left[\binom{D_p}{i}\mathbf 1_E\right] &\le A_h^i\mathbb P(E),\\ \mathbb P(E,\ D_p\ge r) &\le2\mathbb P(E)e^{-r/(4A_h)}. \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Binomial moments of the number of open pivotal edges for the event that the root cluster carries a mark are controlled by a geometric scale, giving an exponential tail.
- **lemma-capped-pivotal-radius-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-good-radius-tail)): For the fixed $\epsilon\in(0,1/2)$, there is $c>0$ such that, uniformly in $x$, all sufficiently large $M$, and integers $1\le K\le M^{1-s}$, $$\label{eq:s08} \mathbb P_{p_c}\left(s_x\le M, \operatorname{rad}_{\mathrm{piv}}(C_x,x)\ge KM^s/2\right) \le\exp\left(-\frac{cK^2M^{2\epsilon}}{\log M}\right).$$
  - Explanation: The probability that a capped critical cluster has large pivotal radius decays like a stretched exponential in the radius scale.
- **lemma-orbit-selection** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-amp-scales)): There are a vertex orbit $O$, a constant $c>0$, and an unbounded sequence $\mathcal N$ of dyadic integers such that, for $n\in\mathcal N$ and $\pi=[\mathbb P_{{p_c}}(n\le s_o<2n)]$, $$\label{eq:s10} \begin{split} \pi&\ge n^{-\beta},\\ [\mathbb P_{{p_c}}(n/2\le s_o<2n)]&\le(1+2^\beta)\pi,\\ \mathbb E_{{p_c}}\bigl[\|C_x\cap O\|\mathbf 1_{\{n\le s_x<2n\}}\bigr] &\ge c n\pi\qquad(x\in O). \end{split}$$ The orbit and the sequence can be kept fixed throughout the amplification.
  - Explanation: Under the temporary divergence assumption on a fractional moment, one orbit and an unbounded dyadic scale sequence carry enough cluster-size mass with regular comparisons between neighbouring scales.
- **prop-efficient-endpoint-maps** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-amp-efficient-maps)): Under <a href="#eq:s09" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:s09">[eq:s09]</a>, for every $\eta>0$ there is a local endpoint map on the orbit $O$ of <a href="#amp:scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="amp:scales">13</a>, with $M\ge2$ and $H>1$, such that $$\frac{U+m}{H}<\eta.$$ Moreover the maps can be chosen so that $H$ tends to infinity. Their radii are finite, but need not be bounded uniformly over the construction.
  - Explanation: If a fractional critical moment were infinite, local endpoint maps would exist whose dispersion parameter dominates the logarithmic success cost plus cap cost, with dispersion tending to infinity.
- **lemma-starting-map** (construction, [source](#stmt-amp-initial)): There is a local endpoint map on $O$ with $H>1$, with cap as large as prescribed.
  - Explanation: A starting local endpoint map is obtained from a lazy walk whose traversed edges are all open, then capped; its norm is small compared with its row sum.
- **lemma-switching-geometry** (construction, [source](#stmt-amp-splicing)): Every step is well defined. Its effect on the distinguished root cluster is exactly $S\mapsto S\cup B_i$. The selected indices increase, and the inserted pieces form a chain, each attached to the preceding piece by one open bridge $e_i$; the first piece is attached to $A$. Every selected bridge separates $x$ from $z$ in the final distinguished field $D'$, and $$\label{eq:s11} z\in C_x(D'),\qquad s_x(D')\le M'=2n+kM.$$
  - Explanation: The switching procedure is well defined; each step enlarges the root cluster by one piece attached through a single open bridge, the seams separate the two endpoints, and the final cluster is bounded in size.
- **lemma-inverse-codes** (bound, [source](#stmt-amp-coding)): Fix the output fields, $x,z$, the old-map seeds, and the priorities ordering contact lists. A valid switching input can be specified by the selected indices and positive gaps locating the selected seams in the ordered list of bridges separating $z$ from $x$, read from $z$ toward $x$. Each fixed code determines at most one input bit tuple, without using the fresh contact uniforms. After integrating those uniforms, the total mass of valid codes is at most $$\label{eq:s12} W^k,\qquad W=1+\sum_{l=1}^{M}\min\{1,2M^s/l\} \le C M^s\log M.$$
  - Explanation: A valid switching input is recovered from selected indices and seam gaps, and, after integrating the contact uniforms, the total mass of valid codes is bounded by a power of a weight that grows only slightly faster than a power of the cap.
- **lemma-mean-load** (bound, [source](#stmt-amp-mean)): With $F(x,z)=\mathbb EL_x(z,D')$, one has $$\label{eq:s13} \begin{split} F(x,z)&=\frac{u^k}{n}\sum_{y\in O} \mathbb P_{{p_c}}(y\in C_x,\ n\le s_x<2n)K^k(z,y),\\ c\pi u^k&\le b_0\le C\pi u^k, \qquad \lVert F/b_0\rVert\le r^k. \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: The mean load kernel has an explicit formula in terms of the old map, its total mass is comparable to the scale probability times the old success power, and the normalized kernel has small norm.
- **lemma-second-moment-load** (bound, [source](#stmt-amp-second-moment)): There is a fixed polynomial $Q(k,M)\ge1$ such that $$\label{eq:s14} \mathbb EL^2\le Q(k,M)\pi u^{2k} \qquad\text{whenever } kM\le n/2 \text{ and } n\ge(W/u)^k.$$
  - Explanation: The second moment of the total load is bounded by a polynomial factor times the expected-load scale, by splitting pairs of inverses into three geometric classes.
- **lemma-amplification-step** (construction, [source](#stmt-amp-step)): Fix an old map. For $n\in\mathcal N$ tending to infinity and $k=O(\log n)$ with $kM\le n/2$ and $n\ge(W/u)^k$, there is, for all sufficiently large such $n$, a new local map with parameters satisfying $$\label{eq:s15} u'\ge c'\pi/Q(k,M),\qquad r'\le2r^k, \qquad M'=2n+kM.$$ The threshold for $n$ may depend on the fixed old map.
  - Explanation: Truncating fields with large load gives a new local map whose success probability is bounded below, whose norm is at most twice a power of the old norm, and whose cap is controlled.
- **lemma-maximal-potential** (bound, [source](#stmt-mom-maximal-potential)): For a deterministic induced domain $D$, put $f_D(v)=\mathbb E_p\lvert C_v^D\rvert$ for $v\in D$, and $f_D(v)=0$ otherwise. Let $\mathcal B^D(v,M)$ be the vertices reachable from $v$ by an open path in $D$ of length at most the nonnegative integer $M$. If $v\in D$ and $0<p<{p_c}$, then $$\mathbb E_p\max_{z\in\mathcal B^D(v,M)}f_D(z) \le \left(1+\frac{1-p}{p}M\right)f_D(v). \label{eq:s16}$$
  - Explanation: The expected maximum of the susceptibility over an intrinsic ball is at most a linear factor in the radius times the susceptibility at the root, below criticality.
- **lemma-map-in-deleted-domain** (bound, [source](#stmt-mom-map-potential)): Fix one of the maps above, with critical success probability $u>0$, cap $M\ge2$, and $H>1$. Run its fixed rule at parameter $p$. On a map-dependent interval $p_0\le p<{p_c}$, with $p_0\ge{p_c}/2$, its success probability $u_p$ and conditional endpoint kernel $K_p$ satisfy $$u_p\ge u/2,\qquad \lVert K_p\rVert\le e^{-(H-1)}.$$ For a deterministic set $A\subseteq V$, put $F_A=f_{V\setminus A}/\chi$, with value zero on $A$. There are constants $Z,C_{\mathrm{map}}$, uniform on this interval, such that $2\le Z\le CM/u$ and, for every $v\in O$, $$(K_pF_A)(v)\le ZF_A(v)+C_{\mathrm{map}}h_A(v), \qquad h_A(v)=\sum_{\substack{a\in A\\\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(v,a)\le M}} \sum_{b\sim a}F_A(b). \label{eq:s17}$$ In the action of $K_p$, functions are restricted to $O$. The constant $C$ depends only on the graph.
  - Explanation: Running a fixed local endpoint map slightly below criticality keeps its success probability and a small kernel norm, and gives a recursive inequality for the normalized potential in a deleted domain.
- **prop-critical-fractional-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mom-critical-moments)): For every $0<\alpha<1/2$, $$\sup_{v\in V}\mathbb E_{{p_c}}s_v^\alpha<\infty. \label{eq:s21}$$
  - Explanation: Cluster sizes at criticality have uniformly bounded fractional moments of every order below one half, by contradiction with the amplified endpoint maps.
- **lemma-decision-tree-covariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mom-decision-tree)): Let $X$ have a finite product law, let $0\le g\le1$, and let an algorithm determine $g(X)$ by querying coordinates without repetition. Write $\delta_i$ for the probability that it queries coordinate $i$, and let $X^{(i)}$ replace just that coordinate by an independent copy. For every real function $f$ on the product space, $$\lvert\mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}(f(X),g(X))\rvert \le\sum_i\delta_i\mathbb E\lvert f(X)-f(X^{(i)})\rvert. \label{mom:covariance}$$ Independent randomization of the algorithm is allowed.
  - Explanation: A covariance bound on finite product spaces between an arbitrary function and a bounded function decided by a non-repeating query algorithm, in terms of query probabilities and resampling influences.
- **prop-susceptibility-almost-linear** (bound, [source](#stmt-mom-susceptibility)): For every $\eta>0$ there is $C_\eta<\infty$ such that $$\chi_{\max}(p)\le C_\eta({p_c}-p)^{-1-\eta}, \qquad 0\le p<{p_c}. \label{eq:s22}$$
  - Explanation: The maximal susceptibility grows below criticality at most like the inverse distance to criticality up to an arbitrarily small extra power.
- **lemma-contacts-two-large-clusters** (bound, [source](#stmt-trace-contact-tail)): Fix $0<\alpha<1/2$ and $\epsilon_1>0$ such that $r=1/2+\epsilon_1-2\alpha<0$. Uniformly for ${p_c}/2\le p<{p_c}$ and $L\ge1$, $$\mathcal D\left(k_e^2 \mathbf 1_{\{\min(\lvert A\rvert,\lvert B\rvert)\ge L\}}\right) \le C L^{1/2+\epsilon_1-2\alpha}. \label{eq:s24}$$
  - Explanation: A type-averaged second moment of the number of closed contacts between two distinct large clusters decays as a power of the size threshold.
- **prop-subpower-triangle** (bound, [source](#stmt-trace-subpower)): For every $\eta>0$ there is $C_\eta<\infty$ such that $$a_3(p)\le C_\eta({p_c}-p)^{-\eta}, \qquad {p_c}/2\le p<{p_c}. \label{eq:s23}$$
  - Explanation: The type-averaged triangle trace grows more slowly than any prescribed power of the inverse distance to criticality.
- **lemma-deformation-basic-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-def-basic)): There is a fixed $C>0$ such that, with $c_k=e^{Ck}$, $$\label{def:edge-weight} \sum_{w:\,e\in E(\gamma_w)}c(w)\le c_k/k \quad\text{for every edge }e.$$ On a connection in a finite cluster, $N^D_{xy}\le c_k\|C_x^D\|$. For fixed $t$, $W_t^D(x,y)$ is increasing under edge openings and under domain enlargement, using restriction of the same configuration. The kernel $T_t$ is invariant, symmetric, and entrywise between zero and $T_0$, and $$\label{def:row-norm} m_t\le\sup_x s_t^V(x)\le c_\sigma\chi_t.$$ The functions $T_t(x,y)$ and $s_t^D(x)$ are continuously differentiable for $t\ge0$, with a right derivative at zero, by differentiating $W_t^D(x,y)$ to $-N^D_{xy}W_t^D(x,y)$ under expectation and summation.
  - Explanation: Template weights summed over an edge are bounded by an exponential in the walk length, and the penalised kernel is symmetric, invariant, dominated by the original kernel and has row-sum norm bounds; derivatives in the deformation time exist.
- **lemma-corridor-calibration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-calibration)): For a template $w$, let $Q_w$ be the full-graph event that the edges of $\gamma_w$ are open and all its blockers are closed. Put $D_w=V\setminus I_w$. Then $Q_w$ is independent of the percolation in $D_w$, and $$\label{def:calibration-identity} c(w)\mathbb P_p(Q_w)=\mu_u(w).$$ On $Q_w$, the template is counted for $x\leftrightarrow y$ if and only if one of the following two exterior events occurs: $$\begin{split} &x\in C_u^{D_w},\quad y\in C_v^{D_w},\quad C_u^{D_w}\ne C_v^{D_w},\\ &x\in C_v^{D_w},\quad y\in C_u^{D_w},\quad C_u^{D_w}\ne C_v^{D_w}. \end{split}$$ In particular, neither terminal belongs to $I_w$.
  - Explanation: The weight of a template times the probability of its forced corridor equals the walk probability of the raw path, and the corridor is counted exactly when two exterior endpoint clusters are distinct.
- **prop-small-norm-change** (bound, [source](#stmt-def-norm)): For every $t\ge0$, $$\label{eq:s27} 0\le T_0-T_t\le 2t\,T_0P^kT_0\quad\text{entrywise},\qquad m_t\ge m_0-2t\rho^km_0^2.$$
  - Explanation: The deformation lowers the connection kernel by at most a term controlled by the walk power sandwiched between connection kernels, so the operator norm drops by a small amount.
- **prop-susceptibility-decrease** (bound, [source](#stmt-def-decrease)): For all $t\ge0$, $$\label{eq:s31} -\chi_t'\ge e^{-tc_k} \left( \mathbb E_{\rm path}\left[ \mathbf 1_{\{\ell_w\ge3\}}s_t^{D_w}(u)s_t^{D_w}(v)\right] -C'(\chi_0^+)^2\rho^ka_3 \right),$$ where $C'>0$ depends only on $G$. The product term is defined to be zero for discarded raw paths.
  - Explanation: The deformed averaged susceptibility decreases at a rate bounded below by the product of exterior endpoint potentials on corridors, up to an error controlled by the triangle trace.
- **lemma-deletion-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-def-deletion)): For $A\subset D$, let $\mathop{\mathrm{Nb}}[A]$ denote $A$ together with all its neighbors in the full graph. For every vertex $v$, $$\label{eq:s28} s_t^{D\setminus A}(v) \ge s_t^D(v)-\chi_0^+\sum_{z\in\mathop{\mathrm{Nb}}[A]}T_0(v,z).$$
  - Explanation: Deleting a vertex set decreases the deformed potential at a vertex by at most the susceptibility times the connection weight to the closed neighbourhood of the deleted set.
- **lemma-two-distinct-clusters** (bound, [source](#stmt-def-correlation)): There is a constant $C'>0$ such that, for every induced domain $D$ and $u,v\in D$, $$\label{eq:s29} \mathbb E_p\bigl(X_t^D(u)X_t^D(v);\ C_u^D\ne C_v^D\bigr) \ge s_t^D(u)s_t^D(v)-C'(\chi_0^+)^2T_0^3(u,v).$$ The constant is independent of $D,p,k,t$.
  - Explanation: On the event that two exterior clusters are distinct, the expected product of deformed potentials is at least the product of the expectations minus a three-step connection error.
- **lemma-two-legs** (bound, [source](#stmt-def-legs)): On $Q_w$, suppose $x\in C_u^{D_w}$, $y\in C_v^{D_w}$, and $C_u^{D_w}\ne C_v^{D_w}$. Then $$\label{eq:s30} N^V_{xy}\le c_k+N^{D_w}_{xu}+N^{D_w}_{vy}.$$
  - Explanation: The penalty of the global connection through a forced corridor splits into a corridor constant plus the penalties of the two exterior legs in the punctured domain.
- **lemma-path-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-def-endpoint)): If a simple path in $D$ joins $b$ to $z$ and has length $l$, then $$\label{eq:s32} s_t^D(b)\ge e^{-tlc_k}p^l s_t^D(z).$$
  - Explanation: Potential at one end of a simple path of length l is bounded below by the potential at the other end times a power of the parameter and an exponential penalty factor.
- **lemma-two-endpoint-escapes** (bound, [source](#stmt-path-selection)): For every $\tau>0$ there are constants $J\in\mathbb N$, $C_1>0$, $k_0\in\mathbb N$, and $b_*>0$, depending only on $G$ and $\tau$, with the following property. For every ${p_c}/2\le p<{p_c}$, every $k\ge k_0$, and every $R\ge1$, a set of paths of $\mathbb E_{\mathrm{path}}$-probability at least $b_*$ has loop-erased length at least three and admits, from each of $u,v$, a simple path in $D_w$ of length at most $$j=J+\left\lceil C_1\log((k+1)R)\right\rceil$$ to a vertex $z$ satisfying $$\sum_{b\in\mathop{\mathrm{Nb}}[I_w]}T_0(z,b)\le\frac{\tau}{R}. \label{eq:s33}$$ The constants $J,C_1,k_0,b_*$ are fixed before $p,k,R$ are chosen.
  - Explanation: For each tolerance there are constants such that a fixed positive share of lazy raw paths have loop-erased length at least three and, from both endpoints, short escape paths in the punctured domain to vertices with small connection weight to the corridor neighbourhood.
- **lemma-connection-smoothing** (bound, [source](#stmt-path-smoothing)): For all vertices $a,b$ and integers $l\ge0$, $$(P^lT_0)(a,b)\le\sqrt{c_\sigma}\,\rho^l. \label{eq:s34}$$
  - Explanation: Applying a power of the walk to the connection kernel gives entries bounded geometrically in the power, using only the walk norm and the finite trace.
- **claim-deformed-row-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-con-row-comparison)): There is $c_f>0$, depending only on $G$, such that $$s_t^V(z)\ge c_f\chi_t \qquad\text{for every $z\in V$ whenever $tc_k\le1$}. \label{eq:s36}$$
  - Explanation: While the deformation time is small, every deformed row sum is bounded below by a fixed proportion of the maximal one.
- **lemma-monotone-endpoint** (theorem, [source](#stmt-con-critical-limit)): Let $G$ be an infinite locally finite graph, let $p_*\in(0,1]$, and let $T_p(x,y)=\mathbb P_p(x\leftrightarrow y)$ be its Bernoulli bond connection kernels. Then $T_{p_*}$ is bounded on counting-measure $\ell^2(V)$ if and only if $\sup_{0\le p<p_*}\lVert T_p\rVert_{2\to2}<\infty$. In that case the supremum equals $\lVert T_{p_*}\rVert_{2\to2}$.
  - Explanation: Boundedness of the connection operator at a parameter is equivalent to uniform boundedness of the operators below it, with equal norms.
- **prop-coupled-interval** (theorem, [source](#stmt-con-coupled-interval)): Let $G$ be infinite, connected, locally finite, and quasi-transitive. Suppose ${p_c}<p_1<p_2<1$ and $T_{p_2}$ is bounded on $\ell^2(V)$. In the coupling obtained by assigning independent uniform labels to edges and opening an edge at parameter $p$ when its label is at most $p$, almost surely there are infinitely many infinite clusters simultaneously at every $p\in[p_1,p_2]$.
  - Explanation: If the connection operator is bounded at the upper endpoint of an interval above criticality, one label coupling yields almost surely infinitely many infinite clusters simultaneously for every parameter in the interval.

```json
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-critical-laws",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph with $h_V(G)>0$. For Bernoulli bond percolation on $G$, $$\\label{eq:critical-triangle}\n \\nabla_{{p_c}}(v)\\le\\lVert T_{{p_c}}\\rVert_{2\\to2}^{3}<\\infty\n \\qquad\\text{for every }v\\in V.$$ For every $v\\in V$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\chi_v(p)&\\asymp({p_c}-p)^{-1} &&(p\\uparrow{p_c}), \\label{eq:meanfield-chi}\\\\\n \\theta_v(p)&\\asymp p-{p_c}&&(p\\downarrow{p_c}), \\label{eq:meanfield-theta}\\\\\n \\mathbb P_{{p_c}}(|C_v|\\ge n)&\\asymp n^{-1/2} &&(n\\to\\infty), \\label{eq:meanfield-size}\\\\\n \\mathbb P_{{p_c}}\\bigl(\\operatorname{rad}_{\\mathrm{int}}(C_v)\\ge n\\bigr)\n    &\\asymp n^{-1} &&(n\\to\\infty), \\label{eq:meanfield-intrinsic}\\\\\n \\mathbb P_{{p_c}}\\bigl(\\operatorname{rad}_{\\mathrm{ext}}(C_v)\\ge n\\bigr)\n    &\\asymp n^{-1} &&(n\\to\\infty). \\label{eq:meanfield-extrinsic}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $n$ tends through positive integers. The comparison constants and the one-sided neighborhoods of ${p_c}$ may depend on $G$ and $v$. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:exponential-threshold}\n {p_c}(G)<{p_{2\\to 2}}(G)\\le{p_{\\exp}}(G).$$ For each fixed $p\\in[0,{p_{2\\to 2}}(G))$, there are constants $0<A_{G,p}<\\infty$ and $a_{G,p}>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:exponential-connectivity}\n T_p(x,y)\\le A_{G,p}e^{-a_{G,p}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_G(x,y)}\n \\qquad\\text{for every }x,y\\in V.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-214.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pre-probability-tools",
        "exact_quote": "Increasing nonnegative functions of independent percolation bits have nonnegative covariance. If increasing events have disjoint finite open witnesses, their joint disjoint-occurrence probability is at most the product of their probabilities. In particular, for all $x,y,z$, $$\\label{eq:s01}\n \\mathbb P_p(x\\leftrightarrow y,\\ x\\leftrightarrow z)\n \\le\\sum_w T_p(x,w)T_p(w,y)T_p(w,z).$$ The same assertions hold in every induced domain.",
        "source_id": "PM-214.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pre-probability-tools",
          "exact_quote": "Increasing nonnegative functions of independent percolation bits have nonnegative covariance.           "source_id": "PM-214.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pre-trace",
        "exact_quote": "For a bounded invariant operator $S$, define $\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}S=[S(o,o)]$. This is a positive normalized trace: $\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}I=1$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(AB)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(BA)$ for bounded invariant $A,B$. If $A$ is positive semidefinite, invariant, and bounded, and $B=B^*$ is invariant and bounded, then $$|\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(BA)|\\le\\lVert B\\rVert\\,\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}A,\n \\qquad A(x,x)\\le c_\\sigma\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}A.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-214.1"
      },

Every selected bridge separates $x$ from $z$ in the final distinguished field $D'$, and $$\\label{eq:s11}\n z\\in C_x(D'),\\qquad s_x(D')\\le M'=2n+kM.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-amp-coding",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the output fields, $x,z$, the old-map seeds, and the priorities ordering contact lists. A valid switching input can be specified by the selected indices and positive gaps locating the selected seams in the ordered list of bridges separating $z$ from $x$, read from $z$ toward $x$. Each fixed code determines at most one input bit tuple, without using the fresh contact uniforms. After integrating those uniforms, the total mass of valid codes is at most $$\\label{eq:s12}\n W^k,\\qquad\n W=1+\\sum_{l=1}^{M}\\min\\{1,2M^s/l\\}\n       \\le C M^s\\log M.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-def-calibration",
        "exact_quote": "For a template $w$, let $Q_w$ be the full-graph event that the edges of $\\gamma_w$ are open and all its blockers are closed. Put $D_w=V\\setminus I_w$. Then $Q_w$ is independent of the percolation in $D_w$, and $$\\label{def:calibration-identity}\n c(w)\\mathbb P_p(Q_w)=\\mu_u(w).$$ On $Q_w$, the template is counted for $x\\leftrightarrow y$ if and only if one of the following two exterior events occurs: $$\\begin{split}\n &x\\in C_u^{D_w},\\quad y\\in C_v^{D_w},\\quad\n                C_u^{D_w}\\ne C_v^{D_w},\\\\\n &x\\in C_v^{D_w},\\quad y\\in C_u^{D_w},\\quad\n                C_u^{D_w}\\ne C_v^{D_w}.\n \\end{split}$$ In particular, neither terminal belongs to $I_w$.",
        "source_id": "PM-214.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-214.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For a template $w$, let $Q_w$ be the full-graph event that the edges of $\\gamma_w$ are open and all its blockers are closed. Put $D_w=V\\setminus I_w$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-path-selection",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\tau>0$ there are constants $J\\in\\mathbb N$, $C_1>0$, $k_0\\in\\mathbb N$, and $b_*>0$, depending only on $G$ and $\\tau$, with the following property. For every ${p_c}/2\\le p<{p_c}$, every $k\\ge k_0$, and every $R\\ge1$, a set of paths of $\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm{path}}$-probability at least $b_*$ has loop-erased length at least three and admits, from each of $u,v$, a simple path in $D_w$ of length at most $$j=J+\\left\\lceil C_1\\log((k+1)R)\\right\\rceil$$ to a vertex $z$ satisfying $$\\sum_{b\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Nb}}[I_w]}T_0(z,b)\\le\\frac{\\tau}{R}.\n \\label{eq:s33}$$ The constants $J,C_1,k_0,b_*$ are fixed before $p,k,R$ are chosen.",
        "source_id": "PM-214.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-path-selection",

          "source_id": "PM-214.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every $\\tau>0$ there are constants $J\\in\\mathbb N$, $C_1>0$, $k_0\\in\\mathbb N$, and $b_*>0$, depending only on $G$ and $\\tau$, with the following property. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **quasi-transitive graph** ([source](#pm-h-3)): It is *quasi-transitive* if its automorphism group $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(G)$ has finitely many orbits on $V$.
- **vertex isoperimetric constant** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a finite nonempty set $A\subset V$, write $$\partial_V A=\{v\in V\setminus A:v\sim a\text{ for some }a\in A\}, \qquad h_V(G)=\inf_{0<\|A\|<\infty}\frac{\|\partial_V A\|}{\|A\|}.$$
- **nonamenable graph** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The graph is *nonamenable* if $h_V(G)>0$.
- **critical and uniqueness thresholds** ([source](#pm-h-3)): If $N_\infty(p)$ is the number of infinite open clusters, define $$\begin{aligned} {p_c}(G)&=\inf\{p\in[0,1]:\mathbb P_p(N_\infty(p)\ge1)>0\},\\ {p_u}(G)&=\inf\{p\in[0,1]:\mathbb P_p(N_\infty(p)=1)=1\}. \end{aligned}$$
- **two-point connection kernel** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The two-point connection kernel is $T_p(x,y)=\mathbb P_p(x\leftrightarrow y)$.
- **operator threshold** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Its threshold is $${p_{2\to 2}}(G)=\sup\{p\in[0,1]:T_p\text{ is bounded on }\ell^2(V)\}.$$
- **susceptibility** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For $p\in[0,1]$, write $$\chi_x(p)=\mathbb E_p\|C_x\|=\sum_yT_p(x,y),\qquad \chi_{\max}(p)=\sup_x\chi_x(p),$$ for the susceptibility and its supremum over vertices.
- **triangle diagram** ([source](#pm-h-6)): The next quantity is the triangle diagram $$\nabla_p(x)=\sum_{y,z}T_p(x,y)T_p(y,z)T_p(z,x).$$
- **cluster notation** ([source](#pre:section)): Write $C_x^D$ for the open cluster of $x$ in $D$, and put $$C_x=C_x^V,\qquad s_x=\|C_x\|,\qquad T_p^D(x,y)=\mathbb P_p(x\leftrightarrow y\text{ in }D),\qquad T_p=T_p^V.$$
- **invariant kernel** ([source](#pre:section)): A kernel is called invariant if its value at $(gx,gy)$ equals its value at $(x,y)$ for every automorphism $g$.
- **type weights and type average** ([source](#pre:types)): Fix a Haar measure on $\Gamma$, and set $$\sigma_i= \frac{\operatorname{vol}(\Gamma_{o_i})^{-1}} {\sum_{j=1}^{h_0}\operatorname{vol}(\Gamma_{o_j})^{-1}}, \qquad [F(o)]=\sum_{i=1}^{h_0}\sigma_iF(o_i), \qquad c_\sigma=(\min_i\sigma_i)^{-1}.$$
- **invariant trace** ([source](#stmt-pre-trace)): For a bounded invariant operator $S$, define $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S=[S(o,o)]$.
- **symmetric lazy walk kernel** ([source](#pre:walk-subsection)): Define $$P(x,y)=\frac1{2d}\quad(x\sim y),\qquad P(x,x)=1-\frac{\deg(x)}{2d},$$ with all other entries zero.
- **bridge** ([source](#stmt-good-definition)): A bridge of a connected open graph is an open edge whose deletion disconnects that graph.
- **pivotal distance** ([source](#stmt-good-definition)): The *pivotal distance* between two vertices is the number of bridges separating them.
- **good cluster for a cap** ([source](#stmt-good-definition)): A finite open cluster $C$ with $\|C\|\le M$ is *good for cap $M$* if the following holds for every set $A\subseteq C$. For every open component $B$ after deleting $A$, let $K$ be the number of original open edges from $B$ to $A$. Whenever $K\ge1$, $$\operatorname{diam}_{\mathrm{piv}}(B)\le K M^s.$$ The pivotal distance in this condition is computed inside $B$.
- **local endpoint map** ([source](#stmt-amp-map)): A local endpoint map on $O$ is a randomized rule at each $x\in O$ that reads percolation bits in a deterministic finite ball about $x$ and uses auxiliary randomness independent of those bits.
- **capped endpoint map** ([source](#stmt-amp-map)): A map with integer cap $M$ is required, on every successful input pattern, to have $s_x\le M$ and $C_x$ good for cap $M$.
- **endpoint map parameters** ([source](#stmt-amp-map)): Write $u>0$ for its success probability and $$\begin{gathered} K(x,y)=\mathbb P(\text{output }y\mid\text{success at }x), \qquad r=\lVert K\rVert,\\ U=\log(1/u),\quad m=\log M,\quad H=\log(1/r). \end{gathered}$$ All probabilities here include the auxiliary randomness, and the operator norm is on counting-measure $\ell^2(O)$.
- **raw path** ([source](#stmt-def-templates)): A raw path is a $k$-step path $w=(w_0,\ldots,w_k)$ of $P$, including possible holding steps.
- **template** ([source](#stmt-def-templates)): Retain $w$ as a *template* precisely when $\ell_w\ge3$.
- **blockers** ([source](#stmt-def-templates)): The *blockers* of a template $w$ are all edges incident to $I_w$ that are not edges of $\gamma_w$; write $b_w$ for their number.
- **template weight** ([source](#stmt-def-templates)): Give this template the weight $$c(w)=\mu_u(w)p^{-\ell_w}q^{-b_w}.$$
- **weighted connection penalty** ([source](#stmt-def-templates)): If $x,y\in D$ are connected, let $N^D_{xy}$ be the sum of $c(w)$ over eligible templates for which every edge of $\gamma_w$ is open and pivotal for $x\leftrightarrow y$ in $D$, and every blocker is closed or absent.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-critical-norm-finite | critical connection operator norm | < | $\infty$ | Critical parameter; equals the supremum of subcritical norms |
| q-critical-below-operator-threshold | critical parameter versus operator threshold | < | ${p_{2\to 2}}(G)$ | Strict gap p_c < p_{2->2} |
| q-operator-threshold-vs-uniqueness | operator threshold | ≤ | ${p_u}(G)$ | No further restriction |
| q-critical-parameter-nontrivial | critical parameter | < | $1$ | Also positive |
| q-walk-norm | operator norm of the lazy walk kernel | < | $1$ | Positive edge isoperimetry |
| q-triangle-critical | critical triangle diagram | ≤ | $\lVert T_{{p_c}}\rVert_{2\to2}^{3}$ | Every vertex |
| q-susceptibility-near-linear | maximal susceptibility | ≤ | $C_\eta({p_c}-p)^{-1-\eta}$ | Parameters below criticality |
| q-triangle-subpower | type-averaged triangle trace | ≤ | $C_\eta({p_c}-p)^{-\eta}$ | Parameters between half the critical value and criticality |
| q-critical-fractional-moment | supremum of critical fractional moments of cluster size | < | $\infty$ | All orders below one half |
| q-bad-cluster-probability | probability of a capped critical cluster that is not good | ≤ | $e^{-M^\epsilon}$ | All sufficiently large caps |
| q-inverse-code-weight | inverse code weight | ≤ | $C M^s\log M$ | Cap at least two |
| q-mean-load-norm | norm of the normalized mean load kernel | ≤ | $r^k$ | Number of old steps k |
| q-second-moment-load | second moment of the total load | ≤ | $Q(k,M)\pi u^{2k}$ | Scale large relative to the old map |
| q-amplified-norm | norm of the amplified endpoint kernel | ≤ | $2r^k$ | k of logarithmic order in the scale |
| q-contact-second-moment | averaged second moment of contacts between large clusters | ≤ | $C L^{1/2+\epsilon_1-2\alpha}$ | Negative exponent |
| q-template-edge-weight | total template weight through an edge | ≤ | $c_k/k$ | Every edge |
| q-deformed-norm-loss | deformed operator norm | ≥ | $m_0-2t\rho^km_0^2$ | All deformation times |
| q-escape-connection-weight | connection weight from an escape endpoint to the corridor neighbourhood | ≤ | $\frac{\tau}{R}$ | Large walk length and any R at least one |
| q-connection-smoothing | smoothed connection kernel entry | ≤ | $\sqrt{c_\sigma}\,\rho^l$ | Subcritical parameters |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-simple-reduction** [claim](#stmt-pre-simple-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-simple-reduction): Subdivide every edge and compare percolation at squared parameter, then transfer isoperimetry and operator boundedness. -> Reduction to simple graphs
- **step-critical-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-pre-critical-finiteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-critical-finiteness): Counting paths and connected sets for the two bounds, exponential growth, then a cited critical-finiteness theorem. -> No infinite clusters at criticality
- **step-product-tools** [claim](#stmt-pre-probability-tools) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-probability-tools): Induction on the last bit for association; finite-product disjoint-witness inequality; union bound over the branch vertex. -> Association, disjoint witnesses, tree bound
- **step-threshold-order** [claim](#stmt-pre-threshold-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-threshold-order): Uniqueness gives uniform positive lower bounds on connections, incompatible with boundedness. -> Operator threshold at most uniqueness threshold
- **step-unimodular-reduction** [claim](#stmt-pre-unimodular-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-unimodular-reduction): Locally compact automorphism group; cited nonunimodular operator theorem; threshold order. -> Strict gap in the nonunimodular case
- **step-mass-transport** [claim](#stmt-pre-mass-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-mass-transport): Orbit decomposition of ordered pairs and equality of conjugate stabilizer volumes. -> Weighted mass transport identity
- **step-trace** [claim](#stmt-pre-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-trace): Diagonal absolute summability, mass transport for cyclicity, positive square root for the trace estimate. -> Normalized invariant trace
- **step-walk-gap** [claim](#stmt-pre-walk) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-walk): Level-set identity with edge isoperimetry and Cauchy-Schwarz; irreducibility of the type chain. -> Walk norm below one, type mixing
- **step-subcritical-susceptibility** [claim](#stmt-pre-sharpness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-sharpness): Finite-set sharpness criterion adapted to vertex types and a differential inequality. -> Finite subcritical susceptibility and type comparison
- **step-russo** [claim](#stmt-pre-russo) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-russo): Finite-domain closed pivotal differentiation, domination, exhaustion, Lebesgue differentiation. -> Russo identities in operator norm
- **step-sprinkling** [claim](#stmt-pre-sprinkling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-sprinkling): Union of two independent fields, decomposition of a path at second-field edges, disjoint-witness bound. -> Boundedness extends above criticality
- **step-marked-pivotals** [claim](#stmt-good-marked-pivotals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-good-marked-pivotals): Exploration with marks, score identity, exponential moment bound, recursion on pivotals. -> Moment and tail bounds for marked pivotals
- **step-radius-tail** [claim](#stmt-good-radius-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-good-radius-tail): Choose a witness vertex, mark it alone, apply the pivotal tail bound with a tuned mark density. -> Capped pivotal-radius tail
- **step-good-clusters** [claim](#stmt-good-good-clusters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-good-radius-tail-2): Close contacts to reduce to the radius tail event, bound the multiplicity of the change, sum over contact counts. -> Stretched-exponential bound on bad clusters
- **step-orbit-selection** [claim](#stmt-amp-scales) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-scales): Record indices of dyadic shell probabilities, transport within clusters to select an orbit. -> Orbit and scale sequence
- **step-starting-map** [claim](#stmt-amp-initial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-initial): Walk conditioned on open edges and corrected by a coin, then capped using goodness. -> A first local endpoint map
- **step-switching-geometry** [claim](#stmt-amp-splicing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-splicing): Induction on the selected indices tracking the root cluster and its single seams. -> Chain geometry of the switched cluster
- **step-inverse-codes** [claim](#stmt-amp-coding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-coding): Encode seams by gaps in the bridge order and use goodness to bound the inverse multiplicity. -> Bound on total code mass
- **step-mean-load** [claim](#stmt-amp-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-mean): Mass preservation of the density comparison and symmetry of the cluster connection kernel. -> Mean load kernel and norm bound
- **step-second-moment** [claim](#stmt-amp-second-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-second-moment): Case split into no switch, nested or adjacent hanging components, and disjoint nonadjacent ones with an injectivity argument. -> Second moment bound
- **step-amplification-step** [claim](#stmt-amp-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-step): Truncate high-load fields by Chebyshev, discard non-good roots, normalize to a new map. -> One amplification step
- **step-efficient-maps** [claim](#stmt-amp-efficient-maps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-step-2): Choose parameters so the ratio contracts each iteration, then iterate with fixed orbit and scales. -> Efficient endpoint maps
- **step-maximal-potential** [claim](#stmt-mom-maximal-potential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mom-maximal-potential): Resample the cluster of a vertex and count entering closed edges as pivotal, then exhaust. -> Ball-maximal potential bound
- **step-map-potential** [claim](#stmt-mom-map-potential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mom-map-potential): Continuity in the parameter of the map kernel and a last-exit decomposition of the chosen path. -> Recursive inequality for the endpoint action
- **step-critical-moments** [claim](#stmt-mom-critical-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mom-critical-moments): Contradiction: a divergent moment gives a differential inequality, hence a susceptibility bound, then a transcript comparison gives a tail bound contradicting divergence. -> Uniform fractional moments
- **step-decision-tree** [claim](#stmt-mom-decision-tree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mom-decision-tree): Hybrid replacement of queried coordinates and telescoping of increments. -> Decision-tree covariance bound
- **step-susceptibility-bound** [claim](#stmt-mom-susceptibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mom-susceptibility): Mark vertices, bound revealment by fractional moments, derive a differential inequality for tails and sum. -> Almost linear susceptibility bound
- **step-contact-tail** [claim](#stmt-trace-contact-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trace-contact-tail): Compare two exploration scores, one in the full graph and one after deleting the first cluster, then rerooting. -> Contact bound for large clusters
- **step-subpower-triangle** [claim](#stmt-trace-subpower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trace-subpower): Differential inequality for the trace split by smaller cluster size, then integration. -> Subpower triangle growth
- **step-deformation-basics** [claim](#stmt-def-basic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-basic): Weight counting over walk steps, monotonicity by comparison of counted templates, dominated convergence for derivatives. -> Deformation bounds and differentiability
- **step-calibration** [claim](#stmt-def-calibration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-calibration): Count specified bits at the corridor interior and analyse exterior bypasses. -> Calibration identity and exterior events
- **step-norm-change** [claim](#stmt-def-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-norm): Differentiate in the time parameter, bound each template by two exterior connections via BK, integrate. -> Small operator norm change
- **step-deletion** [claim](#stmt-def-deletion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-deletion): Single visiting event splitting an old simple path and BK. -> Deletion estimate
- **step-two-clusters** [claim](#stmt-def-correlation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-correlation): Expose one cluster, use fresh exterior bits, apply the deletion estimate and the three-arm bound. -> Distinct-cluster product bound
- **step-two-legs** [claim](#stmt-def-legs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-legs): Separate templates using corridor edges from those inside one exterior cluster. -> Leg decomposition of the penalty
- **step-endpoint-comparison** [claim](#stmt-def-endpoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-endpoint): Condition on the fixed path being open and apply positive association. -> Potential comparison along a path
- **step-susceptibility-decrease** [claim](#stmt-def-decrease) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-legs-2): Expand the derivative over templates, move the root by mass transport and apply the two-cluster and leg estimates. -> Lower bound on the decrease rate
- **step-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-path-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-smoothing): Positive semidefinite Cauchy-Schwarz and the positive trace bound. -> Smoothing bound
- **step-path-selection** [claim](#stmt-path-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-smoothing-2): Labeled experiment with geodesic segments, reversal, domination by the walk law, collision bounds, potential test by Markov. -> Positive share of paths with two escapes
- **step-row-comparison** [claim](#stmt-con-row-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-row-comparison): Connect to a maximal orbit by a short path and apply the path comparison. -> Uniform row comparison
- **step-critical-operator** [claim](#stmt-thm-critical-operator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-smoothing-3): Contradiction: unbounded critical kernel plus sprinkling forces a large norm; choose parameters so the deformation yields a quadratic differential inequality for the susceptibility, incompatible with the retained norm. -> Critical boundedness for unimodular actions
- **step-monotone-endpoint** [claim](#stmt-con-critical-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-critical-limit): Monotone convergence of kernels and extension of a bounded sesquilinear form by density. -> Endpoint norm equality
- **step-coupled-interval** [claim](#stmt-con-coupled-interval) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-coupled-interval): Zero-one law for invariant events, exclusion of one and finitely many merged clusters via boundedness and local resampling. -> Simultaneous infinitely many infinite clusters on an interval
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-coupled-interval-2): Combine critical boundedness, sprinkling, the nonunimodular case, subdivision and the interval proposition. -> Main theorem
- **step-cayley** [claim](#stmt-cor-cayley) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-cayley): Transitive action and Følner criterion, then apply the main theorem. -> Cayley graph corollary
- **step-critical-laws** [claim](#stmt-cor-critical-laws) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-coupled-interval-3): Triangle bound from operator norm, cited mean-field theorems for simple graphs, subdivision transfer and incidence-operator comparison. -> Critical laws and exponential connectivity

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-critical-operator-bounded**: For an infinite connected locally finite simple quasi-transitive nonamenable graph whose automorphism group is unimodular, the critical two-point connection operator is bounded on counting-measure l2.
- **inv-threshold-order**: The operator threshold never exceeds the uniqueness threshold on the quasi-transitive graphs under consideration.
- **inv-main-gap**: On every infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph with positive vertex isoperimetric constant, the critical parameter is strictly below the operator threshold, and an interval of parameters has infinitely many infinite clusters in the uniform-label coupling.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-amenable-graphs**: Apply the nonuniqueness interval and the critical operator bound to a graph with zero vertex isoperimetric constant, such as a Euclidean lattice. -> The theorem needs positive vertex isoperimetry; without it the strict gap and critical boundedness are not asserted.
- **anti-correlated-failures**: Use the results for link failures that are correlated or chosen adversarially. -> The proofs rely on independent edge states, so association and disjoint-witness inequalities are unavailable.
- **anti-finite-networks**: Read the thresholds as sharp transitions for a finite network. -> The thresholds and the infinite-cluster counts are defined for infinite graphs; a finite graph has no infinite cluster.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence and asymptotic statements about Bernoulli percolation on infinite quasi-transitive graphs (operator boundedness, strict threshold gaps, infinitely many infinite clusters). The proofs are analytic and probabilistic, with limits and exhaustion arguments, and give no finite procedure with checkable output.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves existence and boundedness statements about percolation on infinite graphs. It gives no finite procedure that a program could execute, so there is nothing to implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results concern almost-sure behaviour of Bernoulli bond percolation on infinite quasi-transitive graphs. A simulation can only sample finite pieces, so it cannot certify any of the stated thresholds or the interval of nonuniqueness. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No computation with explicit inputs and outputs is offered. The lemmas are analytic inequalities, such as association and disjoint-witness bounds, used inside a proof. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The constructions (local endpoint maps, switching procedures, corridor deformation) are devices inside a contradiction argument on infinite graphs. They are not algorithms with stated inputs, outputs or complexity, so no pseudocode or cost is claimed. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are kernels, type averages and automorphism-invariant traces on infinite vertex sets. No finite storage layout or query structure is defined. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The lazy walk has operator norm below one only on a nonamenable infinite graph. Nothing in the text concerns circuits, memory or parallel evaluation. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Percolation thresholds yield no hardness assumption, primitive or security reduction. Nothing in the statements bears on adversaries or secrecy. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper studies connection probabilities, not learning, data or generalisation. The invariant trace and operator norms are tools for a probabilistic proof, with no training or inference setting. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimisation problem is posed. The thresholds are defined as infima and suprema of probabilities, not as solutions to be computed. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A loose analogy with link failures on overlay graphs exists, but the theorems need an infinite nonamenable quasi-transitive graph with independent edge states. Real deployed systems are finite and their failures are correlated, so no guarantee transfers. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home is probability and geometric group theory, where the result settles a conjecture and feeds later theorems. It is not an engineering contract in a further domain. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-nonamenability-required** (hypothesis): The strict operator gap and the nonuniqueness interval are proved only for graphs with positive vertex isoperimetric constant. Without this hypothesis no conclusion is asserted. Blocked use: ['Applying the interval of infinitely many infinite clusters to amenable graphs such as Euclidean lattices']
- **limit-quasi-transitivity-infinite-graph** (hypothesis): The statements need an infinite, connected, locally finite graph with finitely many vertex orbits. Thresholds and infinite-cluster counts are not meaningful for finite graphs. Blocked use: ['Reading the thresholds as sharp transitions for a finite network', 'Applying type averaging to graphs with infinitely many orbits or unbounded degree']
- **limit-independent-bernoulli-edges** (hypothesis): Every edge is open independently with the same probability. The association and disjoint-witness inequalities rely on this product structure. Blocked use: ['Using the results for correlated or adversarial edge failures']
- **limit-unimodular-new-argument** (formal_scope): The new critical boundedness argument treats a unimodular automorphism group on a simple graph. The nonunimodular case is imported from an existing operator theorem, and loops or multiple edges are handled by subdivision. Blocked use: ['Claiming the new amplification and deformation estimates hold directly for nonunimodular actions or for non-simple graphs']
- **limit-nonconstructive-contradiction** (nonconstructive): Critical boundedness and the fractional moment bounds are proved by contradiction from assumed divergence. No explicit value of the norm or of the constants is produced. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem to compute a numerical bound on the critical norm or on any constant', 'Treating the results as a procedure with stated cost']
- **limit-graph-dependent-interval** (edge_case): The bounds and the interval depend on the graph, and the interval of simultaneous infinitely many infinite clusters is only asserted to be nonempty. Blocked use: ['Assuming a universal width or location of the nonuniqueness interval across graphs']
- **limit-operator-counting-measure** (formal_scope): Operator norms refer to counting-measure l2 on the vertex set. Type weights give a finite trace but do not change the Hilbert space. Blocked use: ['Reading the boundedness result for a weighted or finite-volume norm']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 214 (`lean/docs/214.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Nonuniqueness of percolation on nonamenable quasi-transitive graphs](../../preprints/Nonuniqueness-of-percolation-on-nonamenable-quasi-transitive-graphs-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves the nonuniqueness phase conjecture for Bernoulli bond percolation on infinite connected locally finite nonamenable quasi-transitive graphs. It establishes $p_c<p_{2\to2}\le p_u$ and a common nonempty coupled interval with infinitely many infinite clusters almost surely. For Cayley graphs, the result holds for every finite symmetric generating set of a nonamenable finitely generated group and every $p\in(p_c,p_u)$.
>
> The selected critical estimates include a finite triangle diagram, susceptibility of order $(p_c-p)^{-1}$, percolation probability of order $p-p_c$, cluster-volume tail of order $n^{-1/2}$, and intrinsic and extrinsic radius tails of order $n^{-1}$. Connection probabilities decay exponentially below $p_{2\to2}$.

Comparator source `BenjaminiSchramm.lean` (9159 bytes, sha256 `1751e2795328a58a27f29568bf993f63c29cfeb82d87a80d87038e2428a67a47`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory
open scoped ENNReal NNReal

namespace Percolation
universe u v
variable {V : Type u} {E : Type v}

abbrev BondGraph (V : Type u) (E : Type v) := E → Sym2 V

def Adj (G : BondGraph V E) (x y : V) : Prop := ∃ e, G e = s(x, y)

def LocallyFinite (G : BondGraph V E) : Prop := ∀ x, {e | x ∈ G e}.Finite

def Connected (G : BondGraph V E) : Prop :=
  ∀ x y, Relation.ReflTransGen (Adj G) x y

def IsAut (G : BondGraph V E) (a : V ≃ V) (b : E ≃ E) : Prop :=
  ∀ e, G (b e) = (G e).map a

def QuasiTransitive (G : BondGraph V E) : Prop :=
  ∃ representatives : Finset V, ∀ x, ∃ o ∈ representatives,
    ∃ a : V ≃ V, ∃ b : E ≃ E, IsAut G a b ∧ a o = x

def vertexBoundary (G : BondGraph V E) (A : Finset V) : Set V :=
  {y | y ∉ A ∧ ∃ x ∈ A, Adj G x y}

noncomputable def hV (G : BondGraph V E) : ℝ :=
  sInf {r | ∃ A : Finset V, A.Nonempty ∧
    r = (vertexBoundary G A).ncard / (A.card : ℝ)}

def Conn (G : BondGraph V E) (ω : Set E) (x y : V) : Prop :=
  Relation.ReflTransGen (fun a b => ∃ e ∈ ω, G e = s(a, b)) x y

def cluster (G : BondGraph V E) (ω : Set E) (x : V) : Set V :=
  {y | Conn G ω x y}

def infiniteClusters (G : BondGraph V E) (ω : Set E) : Set (Set V) :=
  {C | C.Infinite ∧ ∃ x, C = cluster G ω x}

noncomputable def law (p : unitInterval) : Measure (Set E) :=
  setBernoulli Set.univ p

noncomputable def pc (G : BondGraph V E) : unitInterval :=
  sInf {p | 0 < law p {ω | (infiniteClusters G ω).Nonempty}}

noncomputable def pu (G : BondGraph V E) : unitInterval :=
  sInf {p | law p {ω | ∃! C, C ∈ infiniteClusters G ω} = 1}

noncomputable def twoPoint (G : BondGraph V E) (p : unitInterval) (x y : V) : ℝ :=
  (law p {ω | Conn G ω x y}).toReal

noncomputable abbrev Hilbert (V : Type u) := lp (fun _ : V => ℂ) 2

noncomputable def HasKernel [DecidableEq V]
    (G : BondGraph V E) (p : unitInterval)
    (A : Hilbert V →L[ℂ] Hilbert V) : Prop :=
  ∀ x y, (A (lp.single 2 y (1 : ℂ))) x = (twoPoint G p x y : ℂ)

noncomputable def OperatorBounded (G : BondGraph V E) (p : unitInterval) : Prop := by
  classical
  exact ∃ A : Hilbert V →L[ℂ] Hilbert V, HasKernel G p A

noncomputable def operatorNorm (G : BondGraph V E) (p : unitInterval) : ℝ≥0∞ := by
  classical
  exact sInf {r | ∃ A : Hilbert V →L[ℂ] Hilbert V,
    HasKernel G p A ∧ r = ENNReal.ofReal ‖A‖}

noncomputable def ptwo (G : BondGraph V E) : unitInterval :=
  sSup {p | OperatorBounded G p}

noncomputable def labelLaw : Measure (E → unitInterval) :=
  Measure.infinitePi (fun _ => (volume : Measure unitInterval))

def openEdges (labels : E → unitInterval) (p : unitInterval) : Set E :=
  {e | labels e ≤ p}

def MainConclusion (G : BondGraph V E) : Prop :=
  (sSup {r | ∃ p : unitInterval, p < pc G ∧ r = operatorNorm G p} =
    operatorNorm G (pc G)) ∧
  operatorNorm G (pc G) < ⊤ ∧
  pc G < ptwo G ∧ ptwo G ≤ pu G ∧
  ∃ p₁ p₂ : unitInterval, pc G < p₁ ∧ p₁ < p₂ ∧ p₂ < 1 ∧
    ∀ᵐ labels ∂(labelLaw : Measure (E → unitInterval)),
      ∀ p ∈ Set.Icc p₁ p₂, (infiniteClusters G (openEdges labels p)).Infinite

end Percolation

run_elab Lean.modifyEnv fun environment => Lean.Meta.auxLemmasExt.setState environment {}

namespace Percolation
universe u v
variable {V : Type u} {E : Type v}
attribute [local instance] Classical.propDecidable

def openSimpleGraph (G : BondGraph V E) (ω : Set E) : SimpleGraph V where
  Adj x y := x ≠ y ∧ ∃ e ∈ ω, G e = s(x,y)
  symm := ⟨by
    rintro x y ⟨hxy, e, he, h⟩
    exact ⟨hxy.symm, e, he, h.trans (Sym2.eq_swap ..)⟩⟩
  loopless := ⟨by simp⟩

end Percolation

run_elab Lean.modifyEnv fun environment => Lean.Meta.auxLemmasExt.setState environment {}

namespace Percolation
universe u v
variable {V : Type u} {E : Type v}
attribute [local instance] Classical.propDecidable Classical.decEq

noncomputable def susceptibility (G : BondGraph V E) (p : unitInterval) (x : V) : ℝ :=
  ∑' y, twoPoint G p x y

end Percolation

run_elab Lean.modifyEnv fun environment => Lean.Meta.auxLemmasExt.setState environment {}

namespace Percolation.BenjaminiSchramm
universe u v
variable {V : Type u} {E : Type v}
attribute [local instance] Classical.decEq

noncomputable def triangleDiagram (G : BondGraph V E) (p : unitInterval) (x : V) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ∑' ab : V × V, ENNReal.ofReal
    (twoPoint G p x ab.1 * twoPoint G p ab.1 ab.2 * twoPoint G p ab.2 x)

noncomputable def graphDistance (G : BondGraph V E) (x y : V) : ℕ :=
  (openSimpleGraph G Set.univ).dist x y

def ExponentialDecay (G : BondGraph V E) (p : unitInterval) : Prop :=
  ∃ A a : ℝ, 0 < A ∧ 0 < a ∧ ∀ x y,
    twoPoint G p x y ≤ A * Real.exp (-a * graphDistance G x y)

noncomputable def pexp (G : BondGraph V E) : unitInterval :=
  sSup {p | ExponentialDecay G p}

def PowerTail (f : ℕ → ℝ) (exponent : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ c C : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ 0 < C ∧ ∀ n : ℕ, 0 < n →
    c * (n : ℝ) ^ (-exponent) ≤ f n ∧ f n ≤ C * (n : ℝ) ^ (-exponent)

def volumeEvent (G : BondGraph V E) (x : V) (n : ℕ) : Set (Set E) :=
  {ω | (n : ℕ∞) ≤ (cluster G ω x).encard}

def intrinsicRadiusEvent (G : BondGraph V E) (x : V) (n : ℕ) : Set (Set E) :=
  {ω | ∃ y ∈ cluster G ω x, n ≤ (openSimpleGraph G ω).dist x y}

def extrinsicRadiusEvent (G : BondGraph V E) (x : V) (n : ℕ) : Set (Set E) :=
  {ω | ∃ y ∈ cluster G ω x, n ≤ graphDistance G x y}

noncomputable def criticalTail (G : BondGraph V E)
    (A : V → ℕ → Set (Set E)) (x : V) (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  (law (pc G) (A x n)).toReal

def CriticalTailLaws (G : BondGraph V E) : Prop :=
  ∀ x, PowerTail (criticalTail G (volumeEvent G) x) (1 / 2) ∧
    PowerTail (criticalTail G (intrinsicRadiusEvent G) x) 1 ∧
    PowerTail (criticalTail G (extrinsicRadiusEvent G) x) 1

def SusceptibilityLaw (G : BondGraph V E) : Prop :=
  ∀ x, ∃ c C δ : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ 0 < C ∧ 0 < δ ∧
    ∀ p : unitInterval, p < pc G → (pc G : ℝ) - p < δ →
      c / ((pc G : ℝ) - p) ≤ susceptibility G p x ∧
      susceptibility G p x ≤ C / ((pc G : ℝ) - p)

noncomputable def percolationProbability (G : BondGraph V E) (p : unitInterval)
    (x : V) : ℝ := (law p {ω | (cluster G ω x).Infinite}).toReal

def PercolationProbabilityLaw (G : BondGraph V E) : Prop :=
  ∀ x, ∃ c C δ : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ 0 < C ∧ 0 < δ ∧
    ∀ p : unitInterval, pc G < p → (p : ℝ) - pc G < δ →
      c * ((p : ℝ) - pc G) ≤ percolationProbability G p x ∧
      percolationProbability G p x ≤ C * ((p : ℝ) - pc G)

end Percolation.BenjaminiSchramm

run_elab Lean.modifyEnv fun environment => Lean.Meta.auxLemmasExt.setState environment {}

namespace Percolation.BenjaminiSchramm
universe u v
variable {V : Type u} {E : Type v}

def CriticalLawsConclusion (G : BondGraph V E) : Prop :=
  (∀ x, triangleDiagram G (pc G) x ≤ operatorNorm G (pc G) ^ 3 ∧
    triangleDiagram G (pc G) x < ⊤) ∧
  SusceptibilityLaw G ∧ PercolationProbabilityLaw G ∧ CriticalTailLaws G ∧
  (pc G < ptwo G ∧ ptwo G ≤ pexp G) ∧
  ∀ p : unitInterval, p < ptwo G → ExponentialDecay G p

end Percolation.BenjaminiSchramm

run_elab Lean.modifyEnv fun environment => Lean.Meta.auxLemmasExt.setState environment {}

namespace Percolation.BenjaminiSchramm
universe u
variable {Λ : Type u} [Group Λ]
attribute [local instance] Classical.decEq

abbrev CayleyEdge (S : Finset Λ) := (SimpleGraph.mulCayley (S : Set Λ)).edgeSet

def cayleyBond (S : Finset Λ) : BondGraph Λ (CayleyEdge S) := Subtype.val

def FolnerAmenable (Λ : Type u) [Group Λ] : Prop :=
  ∀ K : Finset Λ, ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ A : Finset Λ, A.Nonempty ∧
    ∀ k ∈ K, (((A.image (fun x => x * k)) \ A).card : ℝ) < ε * A.card

end Percolation.BenjaminiSchramm

namespace Percolation.BenjaminiSchramm

universe u v

/-- Critical operator boundedness, the threshold gap, and simultaneous nonuniqueness. -/
theorem full_main {V : Type u} {E : Type v} [Infinite V]
    (G : BondGraph V E) (hc : Connected G) (hl : LocallyFinite G)
    (hq : QuasiTransitive G) (hh : 0 < hV G) : MainConclusion G := by
  sorry

/-- Critical mean-field laws and uniform exponential connectivity below the operator threshold. -/
theorem critical_laws {V : Type u} {E : Type v} [Infinite V]
    (G : BondGraph V E) (hc : Connected G) (hl : LocallyFinite G)
    (hq : QuasiTransitive G) (hh : 0 < hV G) : CriticalLawsConclusion G := by
  sorry

/-- Nonuniqueness for every prescribed finite symmetric Cayley generating set. -/
theorem cayley_main {Λ : Type u} [Group Λ]
    (S : Finset Λ) (hsym : ∀ s ∈ S, s⁻¹ ∈ S) (_hone : 1 ∉ S)
    (hgen : Subgroup.closure (S : Set Λ) = ⊤) (hna : ¬ FolnerAmenable Λ) :
    OperatorBounded (cayleyBond S) (pc (cayleyBond S)) ∧
      MainConclusion (cayleyBond S) ∧
      ∀ p : unitInterval, pc (cayleyBond S) < p → p < pu (cayleyBond S) →
        ∀ᵐ ω ∂law p, (infiniteClusters (cayleyBond S) ω).Infinite := by
  sorry

end Percolation.BenjaminiSchramm

end OAI
```

```json
    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Nonuniqueness of percolation on nonamenable quasi-transitive graphs](../../preprints/Nonuniqueness-of-percolation-on-nonamenable-quasi-transitive-graphs-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe formalization proves the nonuniqueness phase conjecture for Bernoulli bond percolation on infinite connected locally finite nonamenable quasi-transitive graphs. It establishes $p_c<p_{2\\to2}\\le p_u$ and a common nonempty coupled interval with infinitely many infinite clusters almost surely. For Cayley graphs, the result holds for every finite symmetric generating set of a nonamenable finitely generated group and every $p\\in(p_c,p_u)$.\n\nThe selected critical estimates include a finite triangle diagram, susceptibility of order $(p_c-p)^{-1}$, percolation probability of order $p-p_c$, cluster-volume tail of order $n^{-1/2}$, and intrinsic and extrinsic radius tails of order $n^{-1}$. Connection probabilities decay exponentially below $p_{2\\to2}$."
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the bond-percolation nonuniqueness conjecture of Benjamini and Schramm: every infinite connected, locally finite, nonamenable quasi-transitive graph has a nonempty interval of parameters for which Bernoulli bond percolation almost surely has infinitely many infinite clusters. We also prove Hutchcroft's stronger operator-threshold conjecture, establishing $`p_c\lt p_{2\to2}\le p_u`$.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph with $h_V(G)>0$. |
